Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). 387: +func ovsVsctl(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {

393: +func ovsInterfaceNames(condition string) ([]string, error) {
394: +	out, err := ovsVsctl(
395: +		"--format=csv",
396: +		"--no-headings",
397: +		"--data=bare",
398: +		"--columns=name",
399: +		"find",
400: +		"Interface",
401: +		condition,
402: +	)
403: +	if err != nil {
404: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("find OVS interfaces: %w (%s)", err, out)
405: +	}
406: +
407: +	text := strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
408: +	if text == "" {
409: +		return nil, nil
410: +	}
411: +
412: +	return strings.Split(text, "\n"), nil
413: +}
414: +
415: +func ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(iface string) (bool, error) {
416: +	out, err := ovsVsctl("--data=bare", "get", "Interface", iface, "error")
417: +	if err != nil {
418: +		// The port disappeared between ListPorts and this get. Nothing left to delete.
419: +		if ovsRowMissing(string(out)) {
420: +			return false, nil
421: +		}
422: +		return false, fmt.Errorf("get OVS interface %q error: %w (%s)", iface, err, out)
423: +	}
424: +
425: +	return ovsDeviceMissing(string(out)), nil
426: +}
427: +
428: +// ovsDeviceMissing reports whether an OVS interface error column says the netdev is gone.
429: +// A live interface returns "[]".
430: +func ovsDeviceMissing(errorColumn string) bool {
431: +	errorColumn = strings.TrimSpace(errorColumn)
432: +
433: +	return strings.Contains(errorColumn, "could not open network device")
434: +}
435: +
436: +func ovsRowMissing(output string) bool {
437: +	return strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(output), "no row")
438: +}
439: +
440: +func ovsMapValue(value string) string {
441: +	if value == "" {
442: +		return `""`
443: +	}
444: +	for _, r := range value {
445: +		switch {
446: +		case r >= 'a' && r <= 'z', r >= 'A' && r <= 'Z', r >= '0' && r <= '9':
447: +		case strings.ContainsRune("-_.:@", r):
448: +		default:
449: +			return fmt.Sprintf("%q", value)
450: +		}
451: +	}
452: +
453: +	return value
454: +}

523: +
524: +func assertReattachKeepsOnePort(t *testing.T, endpoint string) {
525: +	t.Helper()
526: +
527: +	bridge := "br-ovs-leak"
528: +	c := goOvs.New()
529: +	_ = c.VSwitch.DeleteBridge(bridge)
530: +	if err := c.VSwitch.AddBridge(bridge); err != nil {
531: +		t.Skipf("ovsdb is not available: %v", err)
532: +	}
533: +	t.Cleanup(func() {
534: +		_ = goOvs.New().VSwitch.DeleteBridge(bridge)
535: +	})
536: +
537: +	n := &ovs{}
538: +	if err := n.Init(&clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: bridge}); err != nil {
539: +		t.Fatalf("init: %v", err)
540: +	}
541: +	ep := clablinks.NewEndpointBridge(
542: +		clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(n, endpoint, nil),
543: +		false,
544: +	)
545: +	if err := n.AddEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
546: +		t.Fatalf("add endpoint: %v", err)
547: +	}
548: +
549: +	first, peer := addVethPair(t)
550: +	if err := n.AddLinkToContainer(
551: +		context.Background(),
552: +		first,
553: +		clablinks.SetNameMACAndUpInterface(first, ep),
554: +	); err != nil {
555: +		t.Fatalf("add link: %v", err)
556: +	}
557: +	if err := netlink.LinkDel(peer); err != nil {
558: +		t.Fatalf("delete peer: %v", err)
559: +	}
560: +	if ports := dataPorts(t, bridge); len(ports) != 1 {
561: +		t.Fatalf("peer delete left %v, want the stale port", ports)
562: +	}
563: +
564: +	// An untagged port may belong to the operator and is not removed for a topology interface.
565: +	if out, err := ovsVsctl("--no-wait", "add-port", bridge, "operator-port"); err != nil {
566: +		t.Fatalf("add operator port: %v (%s)", err, out)
567: +	}
568: +	if err := n.DeleteBridgePort("operator-port"); err != nil {
569: +		t.Fatalf("delete bridge port: %v", err)
570: +	}
571: +	if ports := dataPorts(t, bridge); len(ports) != 2 {
572: +		t.Fatalf("ports after untagged delete = %v, want the operator port kept", ports)
573: +	}
574: +	if err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(bridge, "operator-port"); err != nil {
575: +		t.Fatalf("delete operator port: %v", err)
576: +	}
577: +
578: +	// A port named after the interface keeps its config across a reattach.
579: +	isNamedPort := dataPorts(t, bridge)[0] == endpoint
580: +	if isNamedPort {
581: +		if out, err := ovsVsctl("set", "Port", endpoint, "tag=100"); err != nil {
582: +			t.Fatalf("set tag: %v (%s)", err, out)
583: +		}
584: +	}
585: +
586: +	second, secondPeer := addVethPair(t)
587: +	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = netlink.LinkDel(secondPeer) })
588: +	if err := n.AddLinkToContainer(
589: +		context.Background(),
590: +		second,
591: +		clablinks.SetNameMACAndUpInterface(second, ep),
592: +	); err != nil {
593: +		t.Fatalf("reattach: %v", err)
594: +	}
595: +	if ports := dataPorts(t, bridge); len(ports) != 1 {
596: +		t.Fatalf("ports after reattach = %v, want 1", ports)
597: +	}
598: +	if isNamedPort {
599: +		out, err := ovsVsctl("get", "Port", endpoint, "tag")
600: +		if err != nil || strings.TrimSpace(string(out)) != "100" {
601: +			t.Fatalf("tag after reattach = %q, err = %v, want 100", out, err)
602: +		}
603: +	}
604: +
605: +	if err := n.Delete(context.Background()); err != nil {
606: +		t.Fatalf("delete node: %v", err)
607: +	}
608: +	if ports := dataPorts(t, bridge); len(ports) != 0 {
609: +		t.Fatalf("ports after node delete = %v", ports)
610: +	}
611: +}
612: +
613: +func addVethPair(t *testing.T) (netlink.Link, netlink.Link) {
614: +	t.Helper()
615: +
616: +	name := clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(nil, "x", nil).GetRandIfaceName()
617: +	peerName := name + "p"
618: +	veth := &netlink.Veth{
619: +		LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{Name: name},
620: +		PeerName:  peerName,
621: +	}
622: +	if err := netlink.LinkAdd(veth); err != nil {
623: +		t.Fatalf("add veth: %v", err)
624: +	}
625: +	link, err := netlink.LinkByName(name)
626: +	if err != nil {
627: +		t.Fatalf("lookup veth: %v", err)
628: +	}
629: +	peer, err := netlink.LinkByName(peerName)
630: +	if err != nil {
631: +		t.Fatalf("lookup peer: %v", err)
632: +	}
633: +
634: +	return link, peer
635: +}
636: +
637: +func dataPorts(t *testing.T, bridge string) []string {
638: +	t.Helper()
639: +
640: +	ports, err := goOvs.New().VSwitch.ListPorts(bridge)
641: +	if err != nil {
642: +		t.Fatalf("list ports: %v", err)
643: +	}
644: +	var data []string
645: +	for _, port := range ports {
646: +		if port != bridge {
647: +			data = append(data, port)
648: +		}
649: +	}
650: +
651: +	return data
652: +}

16: // Endpoint is the interface that all endpoint types implement.
17: // Endpoints like bridge, host, veth and macvlan are the types implementing this interface.
18: type Endpoint interface {
19: 	// Deploy deploys this endpoint through its associated link.
20: 	Deploy(context.Context) error

32: 	GetLink() Link

34: 	Verify(context.Context, *VerifyLinkParams) error
35: 	// HasSameNodeAndInterface returns true if an endpoint that implements this interface

58: 	IfaceName  string
59: 	IfaceAlias string
60: 	// Link is the link this endpoint belongs to.
61: 	Link     Link
62: 	MAC      net.HardwareAddr
63: 	randName string
64: 	IPv4     netip.Prefix
65: 	IPv6     netip.Prefix
66: 	Vars     map[string]any
67: }
68: 
69: func NewEndpointGeneric(node Node, iface string, link Link) *EndpointGeneric {
70: 	return &EndpointGeneric{
71: 		Node:       node,
72: 		IfaceName:  iface,
73: 		IfaceAlias: "",
74: 		// random name is generated for the endpoint to avoid name collisions
75: 		// when it is first deployed in the root namespace
76: 		randName: genRandomIfName(),
77: 		Link:     link,
78: 		Vars:     make(map[string]any),
79: 	}
80: }
81: 
82: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetRandIfaceName() string {
83: 	return e.randName
84: }
85: 
86: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIfaceName() string {
87: 	return e.IfaceName
88: }
89: 
90: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIfaceAlias() string {
91: 	return e.IfaceAlias
92: }
93: 
94: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIfaceDisplayName() string {
95: 	if e.IfaceAlias != "" {
96: 		return e.IfaceAlias
97: 	}
98: 	return e.IfaceName
99: }
100: 
101: func (e *EndpointGeneric) SetIfaceName(ifaceName string) {
102: 	e.IfaceName = ifaceName
103: }
104: 
105: func (e *EndpointGeneric) SetIfaceAlias(ifaceAlias string) {
106: 	e.IfaceAlias = ifaceAlias
107: }
108: 
109: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetMac() net.HardwareAddr {
110: 	return e.MAC
111: }
112: 
113: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIPv4Addr() netip.Prefix {
114: 	return e.IPv4
115: }
116: 
117: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIPv6Addr() netip.Prefix {
118: 	return e.IPv6
119: }
120: 
121: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetVars() map[string]any {
122: 	return e.Vars
123: }
124: 
125: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetLink() Link {
126: 	return e.Link
127: }
128: 
129: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetNode() Node {
130: 	return e.Node
131: }
132: 
133: func (e *EndpointGeneric) SetNode(node Node) {
134: 	e.Node = node
135: }
136: 
137: func (*EndpointGeneric) IsRuntimeDiscovered() bool {
138: 	return false
139: }
140: 
141: func (e *EndpointGeneric) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
142: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
143: 		brSideEp, err := netlink.LinkByName(clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(e.GetIfaceName()))
144: 		_, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError)
145: 
146: 		switch {
147: 		case notfound:
148: 			// The netdev may already be gone while its bridge port is not, which is how OVS
149: 			// keeps a port whose name is not the topology interface name.
150: 			return removeBridgePort(e.GetNode(), e.GetIfaceName())
151: 		case err != nil:
152: 			return err
153: 		}
154: 		log.Debugf(
155: 			"Removing interface %q from namespace %q",
156: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
157: 			e.GetNode().GetShortName(),
158: 		)
159: 		if err = netlink.LinkDel(brSideEp); err != nil {
160: 			return err
161: 		}
162: 
163: 		return removeBridgePort(e.GetNode(), e.GetIfaceName())
164: 	})
165: }
166: 
167: // moveEndpoint moves the endpoint's interface into dst's namespace and transfers ownership.
168: // The interface is left in the DOWN state; callers that need it active should follow up with
169: // Endpoint.Activate once all endpoints are in place.
170: func moveEndpoint(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint, dst Node) error {
171: 	src := e.GetNode()
172: 	if src == nil {
173: 		return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %q has no source node", e.GetIfaceName())
174: 	}
175: 
176: 	if dst == nil {
177: 		return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %q has no destination node", e.GetIfaceName())
178: 	}
179: 
180: 	if src == dst {
181: 		return nil
182: 	}
183: 
184: 	if !slices.Contains(src.GetEndpoints(), e) {
185: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q does not own endpoint %q", src.GetShortName(), e.GetIfaceName())
186: 	}
187: 
188: 	for _, owned := range dst.GetEndpoints() {
189: 		if owned == e {
190: 			return fmt.Errorf(
191: 				"node %q already owns endpoint %q",
192: 				dst.GetShortName(),
193: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
194: 			)
195: 		}
196: 		if owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName() {
197: 			return fmt.Errorf(
198: 				"node %q already tracks interface %q",
199: 				dst.GetShortName(),
200: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
201: 			)
202: 		}
203: 	}
204: 
205: 	if err := ensureOwnershipAltName(ctx, e); err != nil {
206: 		return err
207: 	}
208: 
209: 	if err := moveLink(ctx, src, e.GetIfaceName(), dst); err != nil {
210: 		return err
211: 	}
212: 
213: 	if err := src.ReleaseEndpoint(e); err != nil {
214: 		return err
215: 	}
216: 	e.SetNode(dst)
217: 	if err := dst.AdoptEndpoint(e); err != nil {
218: 		e.SetNode(src)
219: 		_ = src.AdoptEndpoint(e)
220: 		return fmt.Errorf(
221: 			"endpoint %q moved but destination ownership update failed: %w",
222: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
223: 			err,
224: 		)
225: 	}
226: 
227: 	return nil
228: }
229: 
230: // Activate brings the endpoint's interface up in its current namespace.
231: func (e *EndpointGeneric) Activate(ctx context.Context) error {
232: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
233: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
234: 		if err != nil {
235: 			return err
236: 		}
237: 		return netlink.LinkSetUp(link)
238: 	})
239: }
240: 
241: func ensureOwnershipAltName(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint) error {
242: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
243: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
244: 		if err != nil {
245: 			return err
246: 		}
247: 
248: 		return addOwnershipAltName(link, e)
249: 	})
250: }
251: 
252: // HasSameNodeAndInterface returns true if the given endpoint has the same node and interface name
253: // as the `ept` endpoint.
254: func (e *EndpointGeneric) HasSameNodeAndInterface(ept Endpoint) bool {
255: 	return e.Node == ept.GetNode() && e.IfaceName == ept.GetIfaceName()
256: }
257: 
258: func (e *EndpointGeneric) String() string {
259: 	ifDisplayName := e.IfaceName
260: 	if e.IfaceAlias != "" {
261: 		ifDisplayName += fmt.Sprintf(" (%s)", e.IfaceAlias)
262: 	}
263: 	return fmt.Sprintf("%s:%s", e.Node.GetShortName(), ifDisplayName)
264: }
265: 
266: // CheckEndpointUniqueness checks that the given endpoint appears only once for the node
267: // it is assigned to.
268: func CheckEndpointUniqueness(e Endpoint) error {
269: 	for _, ept := range e.GetNode().GetEndpoints() {
270: 		if e == ept {
271: 			// since node contains all endpoints including the one we are checking
272: 			// we skip it
273: 			continue
274: 		}
275: 		// if `e` has the same node and interface name as `ept` then we have a duplicate
276: 		if e.HasSameNodeAndInterface(ept) {
277: 			return fmt.Errorf("duplicate endpoint %s", e)
278: 		}
279: 	}
280: 
281: 	return nil
282: }
283: 
284: // CheckEndpointExists checks that a certain
285: // interface exists in the network namespace of the given node.
286: func CheckEndpointExists(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint) error {
287: 	err := CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet(ctx, e)
288: 	if err == nil {
289: 		return fmt.Errorf("interface %q does not exist", e.String())
290: 	}
291: 	return nil
292: }
293: 
294: // CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet verifies that the interface referenced in the
295: // provided endpoint does not yet exist in the referenced node.
296: func CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint) error {
297: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
298: 		// we expect a netlink.LinkNotFoundError when querying for
299: 		// the interface with the given endpoints name
300: 		var err error
301: 		_, err = netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
302: 
303: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
304: 			return nil
305: 		}
306: 
307: 		return fmt.Errorf(
308: 			"interface %s is defined via topology but already exists: %v",
309: 			e.String(),
310: 			err,
311: 		)
312: 	})
313: }
314: 
315: func moveLink(ctx context.Context, src Node, ifaceName string, dst Node) error {
316: 	return src.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
317: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
318: 		if err != nil {
319: 			return err
320: 		}
321: 
322: 		if err := netlink.LinkSetDown(link); err != nil {
323: 			return err
324: 		}
325: 
326: 		return dst.AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
327: 			return nil
328: 		})
329: 	})
330: }

(End of file - total 330 lines)

51: 		if err != nil {
52: 			return err
53: 		}

55: 		for _, link := range links {

57: 			if name == "lo" || name == "eth0" {
58: 				continue
59: 			}
60: 			if knownIfaceNames != nil {
61: 				if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known {
62: 					continue
63: 				}
64: 			}
65: 			if !isOwnedBy(link, node, ownerName, name) {
66: 				continue
67: 			}
68: 
69: 			info := OwnedInterface{

71: 				Index:       link.Attrs().Index,
72: 				MasterIndex: link.Attrs().MasterIndex,
73: 				Type:        link.Type(),
74: 			}
75: 			if info.MasterIndex != 0 {
76: 				master, masterErr := netlink.LinkByIndex(info.MasterIndex)
77: 				if masterErr != nil {
78: 					return fmt.Errorf(
79: 						"failed to discover master for %q: %w",
80: 						name,
81: 						masterErr,
82: 					)
83: 				}
84: 				info.MasterName = master.Attrs().Name
85: 			}
86: 			if info.Type == "veth" {
87: 				veth, ok := link.(*netlink.Veth)
88: 				if !ok {
89: 					veth = &netlink.Veth{LinkAttrs: *link.Attrs()}
90: 				}
91: 				peerIndex, peerErr := netlink.VethPeerIndex(veth)
92: 				if peerErr != nil {
93: 					return fmt.Errorf("failed to discover veth peer for %q: %w", name, peerErr)
94: 				}
95: 				info.PeerIndex = peerIndex
96: 			}
97: 
98: 			interfaces = append(interfaces, info)
99: 		}
100: 
101: 		return nil
102: 	})
103: 	if err != nil {
104: 		return nil, err
105: 	}
106:

108: 		return interfaces[i].Name < interfaces[j].Name
109: 	})

112: }

133: // ValidateOwnedInterface returns an error when an existing interface is not

140: // a possibly different logical owner. Parking nodes use this when the
141: // interface remains owned by the original node.
142: func ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor(
143: 	ctx context.Context,
144: 	node Node,
145: 	ownerName string,
146: 	ifaceName string,
147: ) error {
148: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
149: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
150: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
151: 			return nil
152: 		}
153: 		if err != nil {
154: 			return err
155: 		}
156: 		if isOwnedBy(link, node, ownerName, ifaceName) {
157: 			return nil
158: 		}
159: 		return fmt.Errorf(
160: 			"interface %q on node %q exists but is not containerlab-owned",
161: 			ifaceName,
162: 			node.GetShortName(),
163: 		)
164: 	})
165: }
166: 
167: func RemoveOwnedInterface(ctx context.Context, node Node, ifaceName string) error {
168: 	if node == nil || ifaceName == "" {
169: 		return nil
170: 	}
171: 
172: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
173: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(ifaceName))
174: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
175: 			return removeBridgePort(node, ifaceName)
176: 		}
177: 		if err != nil {
178: 			return err
179: 		}
180: 		if !isOwnedBy(link, node, ownershipName(node), ifaceName) {
181: 			return fmt.Errorf(
182: 				"%w: interface %q on node %q",
183: 				ErrInterfaceUnowned,
184: 				ifaceName,
185: 				node.GetShortName(),
186: 			)
187: 		}
188: 
189: 		if err = netlink.LinkDel(link); err != nil {
190: 			return err
191: 		}
192: 
193: 		return removeBridgePort(node, ifaceName)
194: 	})
195: }
196: 
197: // bridgePortDeleter removes a bridge port that can outlive its netdev. OVS ports created for an
198: // interface name Linux will not accept are named after the random netdev, so deleting the veth
199: // from the container side leaves the port behind.
200: type bridgePortDeleter interface {
201: 	DeleteBridgePort(string) error
202: }
203: 
204: func removeBridgePort(node Node, ifaceName string) error {
205: 	if node == nil || ifaceName == "" {
206: 		return nil
207: 	}
208: 
209: 	cleaner, ok := node.(bridgePortDeleter)
210: 	if !ok {
211: 		return nil
212: 	}
213: 
214: 	// Best-effort, matching ovs.Delete. A missing bridge or a down ovsdb must not abort
215: 	// destroy or reconcile and leave the container running.
216: 	if err := cleaner.DeleteBridgePort(ifaceName); err != nil {
217: 		log.Errorf(
218: 			"Could not remove bridge port %q on node %q: %v",
219: 			ifaceName,
220: 			node.GetShortName(),
221: 			err,
222: 		)
223: 	}
224:

226: }
227: 
228: // isOwnedBy reports whether link carries the ownership marker of the logical
229: // endpoint ownerName:ifaceName. Interfaces of mgmt-net links deployed by earlier
230: // releases carry a marker for the bridge name of the mgmt-net link node instead
231: // of the stable mgmt-net owner name, and are accepted as well.
232: func isOwnedBy(link netlink.Link, node Node, ownerName, ifaceName string) bool {

234: 		return true

238: 		return HasOwnershipAltNameFor(link, node.GetShortName(), ifaceName)
239: 	}
240: 
241: 	return false

28: const (
29: 	ovsEndpointExtID = "containerlab-endpoint"
30: 	// ovsTimeoutSeconds bounds ovs-vsctl calls, which wait forever when ovs-vswitchd is down.
31: 	ovsTimeoutSeconds = 30
32: )
33: 
34: // clabAutoPort matches the random netdev name used when the topology interface name is not a
35: // valid Linux name. Those netdevs are what Open vSwitch records as the port name.
36: var clabAutoPort = regexp.MustCompile(`^clab-[0-9a-f]{8}$`)

38: var kindNames = []string{"ovs-bridge"}
39: 
40: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
41: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
42: 	r.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {
43: 		return new(ovs)
44: 	}, nil)
45: }

47: type ovs struct {
48: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
49: }

51: func (n *ovs) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
52: 	// Init DefaultNode
53: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
54: 
55: 	n.Cfg = cfg
56: 	for _, o := range opts {
57: 		o(n)
58: 	}
59: 
60: 	n.Cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased = true
61: 
62: 	return nil
63: }
64: 
65: func (n *ovs) CheckDeploymentConditions(_ context.Context) error {
66: 	// check if ovs bridge exists
67: 	c := goOvs.New()
68: 
69: 	// We were previously doing c.VSwitch.Get.Bridge() but it doesn't work
70: 	// when the bridge has a protocol version higher than 1.0
71: 	// So listing the bridges is safer
72: 	bridges, err := c.VSwitch.ListBridges()
73: 	if err != nil {
74: 		return fmt.Errorf("error while listing ovs bridges: %v", err)
75: 	}
76: 	if !clabinternalslices.Contains(bridges, n.Cfg.ShortName) {
77: 		return fmt.Errorf("could not find ovs bridge %q", n.Cfg.ShortName)
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	return nil
81: }
82: 
83: func (n *ovs) Deploy(_ context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
84: 	n.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
85: 	return nil
86: }
87: 
88: func (*ovs) PullImage(_ context.Context) error             { return nil }
89: func (*ovs) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string { return map[string]string{} }
90: 
91: func (n *ovs) Delete(_ context.Context) error {
92: 	c := goOvs.New(goOvs.Timeout(ovsTimeoutSeconds))
93: 
94: 	for _, ep := range n.GetEndpoints() {
95: 		// Under the hood, this is called with "--if-exists", so it will handle the case where it
96: 		// doesn't exist for some reason.
97: 		err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, ep.GetIfaceName())
98: 		if err == nil {
99: 			err = n.removeEndpointPorts(ep.GetIfaceName())
100: 		}
101: 		if err != nil {
102: 			log.Errorf(
103: 				"Could not remove OVS port %q from bridge %q: %v",
104: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
105: 				n.Config().ShortName,
106: 				err,
107: 			)
108: 		}
109: 	}
110: 
111: 	// One sweep for ports created before they were tagged. Per-endpoint deletion already
112: 	// removes ports this version tagged at attach time.
113: 	if err := n.removeMissingAutoPorts(); err != nil {
114: 		log.Errorf(
115: 			"Could not remove stale OVS ports from bridge %q: %v",
116: 			n.Config().ShortName,
117: 			err,
118: 		)
119: 	}
120: 
121: 	return nil
122: }
123: 
124: // DeleteBridgePort removes the OVS port containerlab tagged for one topology interface. Untagged
125: // ports may belong to the operator, so only Delete removes a port by its name.
126: func (n *ovs) DeleteBridgePort(endpoint string) error {
127: 	return n.removeEndpointPorts(endpoint)
128: }

130: func (*ovs) DeleteNetnsSymlink() (err error) { return nil }
131: 
132: // UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo is a noop for bridges.
133: func (*ovs) UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo(_ context.Context) error { return nil }
134: 
135: // GetContainers is a noop for bridges.
136: func (*ovs) GetContainers(_ context.Context) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
137: 	return nil, nil
138: }
139: 
140: // RunExec is noop for ovs kind.
141: func (n *ovs) RunExec(_ context.Context, _ *clabexec.ExecCmd) (*clabexec.ExecResult, error) {
142: 	log.Warnf("Exec operation is not implemented for kind %q", n.Config().Kind)
143: 
144: 	return nil, clabexec.ErrRunExecNotSupported
145: }
146: 
147: func (n *ovs) AddLinkToContainer(
148: 	ctx context.Context,
149: 	link netlink.Link,
150: 	f func(ns.NetNS) error,
151: ) error {
152: 	// retrieve the namespace handle
153: 	curNamespace, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
154: 	if err != nil {
155: 		return err
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	// execute the given function
159: 	err = curNamespace.Do(f)
160: 	if err != nil {
161: 		return err
162: 	}
163: 
164: 	// need to reread the link, due to the changed the function f might have applied to
165: 	// the link. Usually it renames the link to its final name.
166: 	link, err = netlink.LinkByIndex(link.Attrs().Index)
167: 	if err != nil {
168: 		return err
169: 	}
170: 
171: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
172: 	logical := n.logicalEndpointName(link)
173: 	args := []string{"--may-exist", "add-port", n.Cfg.ShortName, name}
174: 	// A port named after the interface is reused by --may-exist and keeps its config. A random
175: 	// netdev name needs a tag, so that a later attach or link removal can find the port. The full
176: 	// stale-port sweep runs once from Delete, not on every attach.
177: 	if logical != name {
178: 		if err := n.removeEndpointPorts(logical); err != nil {
179: 			return err
180: 		}
181: 		args = append(args,
182: 			"--", "set", "Interface", name,
183: 			"external_ids:"+ovsEndpointExtID+"="+ovsMapValue(logical),
184: 		)
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	out, err := ovsVsctl(args...)
188: 	if err != nil {
189: 		return fmt.Errorf("add OVS port %q as %q: %w (%s)", name, logical, err, out)
190: 	}
191: 
192: 	return nil
193: }
194: 
195: func (m *ovs) GetLinkEndpointType() clablinks.LinkEndpointType {
196: 	return clablinks.LinkEndpointTypeBridge
197: }
198: 
199: // logicalEndpointName is the topology interface name for link. When that name is not a valid
200: // Linux interface name, the netdev keeps a random clab-* name and the topology name is an altname.
201: func (n *ovs) logicalEndpointName(link netlink.Link) string {
202: 	if link == nil {
203: 		return ""
204: 	}
205: 
206: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
207: 	alts := make(map[string]struct{}, len(link.Attrs().AltNames))
208: 	for _, alt := range link.Attrs().AltNames {
209: 		alts[alt] = struct{}{}
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	for _, ep := range n.GetEndpoints() {
213: 		iface := ep.GetIfaceName()
214: 		if iface == name {
215: 			return iface
216: 		}
217: 		if _, ok := alts[clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(iface)]; ok {
218: 			return iface
219: 		}
220: 	}
221: 
222: 	return name
223: }
224: 
225: // removeEndpointPorts deletes the ports on this bridge tagged with the topology interface name.
226: func (n *ovs) removeEndpointPorts(endpoint string) error {
227: 	if endpoint == "" || n.Cfg == nil || n.Cfg.ShortName == "" {
228: 		return nil
229: 	}
230: 
231: 	c := goOvs.New(goOvs.Timeout(ovsTimeoutSeconds))
232: 
233: 	names, err := ovsInterfaceNames(
234: 		"external_ids:" + ovsEndpointExtID + "=" + ovsMapValue(endpoint),
235: 	)
236: 	if err != nil {
237: 		return err
238: 	}
239: 
240: 	onBridge, err := bridgePortSet(c, n.Cfg.ShortName)
241: 	if err != nil {
242: 		return err
243: 	}
244: 
245: 	for _, name := range names {
246: 		if _, ok := onBridge[name]; !ok {
247: 			continue
248: 		}
249: 		if err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, name); err != nil {
250: 			return err
251: 		}
252: 	}
253: 
254: 	return nil
255: }
256: 
257: // removeMissingAutoPorts deletes clab-* ports whose netdev is already gone. Deleting the container
258: // side of the veth removes the device and leaves the OVS port in place under that random name.
259: func (n *ovs) removeMissingAutoPorts() error {
260: 	if n.Cfg == nil || n.Cfg.ShortName == "" {
261: 		return nil
262: 	}
263: 
264: 	c := goOvs.New(goOvs.Timeout(ovsTimeoutSeconds))
265: 	ports, err := c.VSwitch.ListPorts(n.Cfg.ShortName)
266: 	if err != nil {
267: 		return err
268: 	}
269: 
270: 	for _, port := range ports {
271: 		if !clabAutoPort.MatchString(port) {
272: 			continue
273: 		}
274: 		missing, err := ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(port)
275: 		if err != nil {
276: 			return err
277: 		}
278: 		if !missing {
279: 			continue
280: 		}
281: 		log.Debugf(
282: 			"Removing stale OVS port %q from bridge %q",
283: 			port,
284: 			n.Cfg.ShortName,
285: 		)
286: 		if err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, port); err != nil {
287: 			return err
288: 		}
289: 	}
290: 
291: 	return nil
292: }
293: 
294: func bridgePortSet(c *goOvs.Client, bridge string) (map[string]struct{}, error) {
295: 	ports, err := c.VSwitch.ListPorts(bridge)
296: 	if err != nil {
297: 		return nil, err
298: 	}
299: 
300: 	set := make(map[string]struct{}, len(ports))
301: 	for _, port := range ports {
302: 		set[port] = struct{}{}
303: 	}
304: 
305: 	return set, nil
306: }
307: 
308: func ovsVsctl(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {
309: 	args = append([]string{fmt.Sprintf("--timeout=%d", ovsTimeoutSeconds)}, args...)
310: 
311: 	return exec.Command("ovs-vsctl", args...).CombinedOutput()
312: }
313: 
314: func ovsInterfaceNames(condition string) ([]string, error) {
315: 	out, err := ovsVsctl(
316: 		"--format=csv",
317: 		"--no-headings",
318: 		"--data=bare",
319: 		"--columns=name",
320: 		"find",
321: 		"Interface",
322: 		condition,
323: 	)
324: 	if err != nil {
325: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("find OVS interfaces: %w (%s)", err, out)
326: 	}
327: 
328: 	text := strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
329: 	if text == "" {
330: 		return nil, nil
331: 	}
332: 
333: 	return strings.Split(text, "\n"), nil
334: }
335: 
336: func ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(iface string) (bool, error) {
337: 	out, err := ovsVsctl("--data=bare", "get", "Interface", iface, "error")
338: 	if err != nil {
339: 		// The port disappeared between ListPorts and this get. Nothing left to delete.
340: 		if ovsRowMissing(string(out)) {
341: 			return false, nil
342: 		}
343: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("get OVS interface %q error: %w (%s)", iface, err, out)
344: 	}
345: 
346: 	return ovsDeviceMissing(string(out)), nil
347: }
348: 
349: // ovsDeviceMissing reports whether an OVS interface error column says the netdev is gone.
350: // A live interface returns "[]".
351: func ovsDeviceMissing(errorColumn string) bool {
352: 	errorColumn = strings.TrimSpace(errorColumn)
353: 
354: 	return strings.Contains(errorColumn, "could not open network device")
355: }
356: 
357: func ovsRowMissing(output string) bool {
358: 	return strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(output), "no row")
359: }
360: 
361: func ovsMapValue(value string) string {
362: 	if value == "" {
363: 		return `""`
364: 	}
365: 	for _, r := range value {
366: 		switch {
367: 		case r >= 'a' && r <= 'z', r >= 'A' && r <= 'Z', r >= '0' && r <= '9':
368: 		case strings.ContainsRune("-_.:@", r):
369: 		default:
370: 			return fmt.Sprintf("%q", value)
371: 		}
372: 	}
373: 
374: 	return value
375: }

(End of file - total 375 lines)

54: 
55: 	// We were previously doing c.VSwitch.Get.Bridge() but it doesn't work
56: 	// when the bridge has a protocol version higher than 1.0
57: 	// So listing the bridges is safer

59: 	if err != nil {

Reasoning
Now I understand the core change. Let me trace key flows:

1. How OVS endpoints get attached: which code calls `AddLinkToContainer` and with what `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` — whether the interface gets renamed to the topology name, and what happens when the topology name is not a valid Linux name (IFNAMSIZ limit 15 chars). Where is the random `clab-*` name generated? Let's look at `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` and `genRandomIfName`.

2. `removeBridgePort` is called from `EndpointGeneric.Remove` inside the node's namespace ExecFunction — but for OVS node, the node is root-namespace-based (`IsRootNamespaceBased = true`). `DeleteBridgePort` runs `ovs-vsctl` in the host — fine since ovs node is root ns based. But for container nodes with OVS peer... wait, `removeBridgePort` is called on `e.GetNode()` — the node whose endpoint is being removed. For a veth endpoint connected to an OVS bridge, the endpoint's node is the container (e.g., srl node), not the ovs node. So `removeBridgePort(node, ...)` type-asserts the container node — which won't implement `DeleteBridgePort`. So when is it useful? Only when the node itself is the OVS node. Hmm, but the ovs.go Delete handles per-endpoint deletion already. What about endpoints whose node is the ovs node — EndpointGeneric.Remove for the ovs node's endpoints? Actually for the OVS bridge endpoint, the netdev in the root namespace... Actually the OVS bridge's endpoints are bridge endpoints; `EndpointGeneric.Remove` would try LinkByName of the bridge name? Let me look at how ovs-bridge endpoints work.

Wait — actually, `removeBridgePort` on a container node: the interface name is looked up via `netlink.LinkByName(SanitizeInterfaceName(ifaceName))`. If not found (netdev gone but OVS port remains), `removeBridgePort(node, ifaceName)` is called with the container's node — the container node doesn't implement `DeleteBridgePort`, so nothing happens. Hmm, but wait: who implements `DeleteBridgePort`? Only `*ovs`. So the bridge-port cleanup in links/endpoint.go Remove is only effective when the removed endpoint's node is the ovs node.

Hmm — but wait, which endpoint gets removed in destroy? Let me look at how link removal happens. In `destroy`, links are removed; for veth links both endpoints are removed. For an OVS-connected veth: endpoint A = container node's interface, endpoint B = ovs node's bridge endpoint (BridgeEndpoint). When removing the container-side veth, the peer (attached to OVS) disappears too — the OVS port under the random name remains if the name was invalid. So `removeBridgePort` being called with the container node does nothing — the cleanup must happen on the ovs node. Let me check how the ovs node's endpoints Remove works. The bridge endpoint for OVS node — `NewEndpointBridge`? Does BridgeEndpoint have its own Remove? Let me look at links/endpoint_bridge.go.

Also `RemoveOwnedInterface` — used where? Let's grep for callers.

Let me also check `GetRandIfaceName`/`genRandomIfName` — pattern `clab-[0-9a-f]{8}`? Let me check.

Also check where altnames are set (ownership altname via `addOwnershipAltName`), and where iface rename happens if name too long — maybe `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` handles it.

Let me search.
Now let me trace the callers and related helpers: how OVS endpoints are attached, where `RemoveOwnedInterface` is called, and how interface naming works.

24: func (e *EndpointBridge) Verify(ctx context.Context, p *VerifyLinkParams) error {
25: 	var errs []error
26: 	err := CheckEndpointUniqueness(e)
27: 	if err != nil {
28: 		errs = append(errs, err)
29: 	}
30: 	// the bridge of a management network that does not exist yet is only known

48: 		return errors.Join(errs...)

60: 	return e.isMgmtBridgeEndpoint
61: }

63: // CheckBridgeExists verifies that the given bridge is present in the
64: // network namespace referenced via the provided nspath handle.
65: func CheckBridgeExists(ctx context.Context, n Node) error {
66: 	return n.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
67: 		br, err := netlink.LinkByName(n.GetShortName())
68: 		_, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError)
69: 		switch {
70: 		case notfound:
71: 			return fmt.Errorf(
72: 				"bridge %q referenced in topology but does not exist",
73: 				n.GetShortName(),
74: 			)

76: 			return err
77: 		case br.Type() != "bridge" && br.Type() != "openvswitch":
78: 			return fmt.Errorf(
79: 				"interface %s found. expected type \"bridge\" or \"openvswitch\", actual is %q",

81: 				br.Type(),

24: // Deploy deploys the link. Endpoint is the endpoint that triggers the creation of the link.

26: 	err := endpoint.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(nn ns.NetNS) error {
27: 		// add the bridge
28: 		err := netlink.LinkAdd(&netlink.Bridge{
29: 			LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{
30: 				Name: b.endpoint.GetIfaceName(),
31: 			},
32: 		})

34: 			return err
35: 		}

55: 	if b.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
56: 		return nil

65: 	return nil

1: package links
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"fmt"
5: 	"net"
6: 	"net/netip"
7:

9: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
10: )
11: 
12: // EndpointRaw is the raw (string) representation of an endpoint as defined in the topology file
13: // for a given link definition.
14: type EndpointRaw struct {
15: 	Node  string         `yaml:"node"`
16: 	Iface string         `yaml:"interface"`
17: 	MAC   string         `yaml:"mac,omitempty"`
18: 	IPv4  string         `yaml:"ipv4,omitempty"`
19: 	IPv6  string         `yaml:"ipv6,omitempty"`
20: 	Vars  map[string]any `yaml:"vars,omitempty"`
21: }
22: 
23: // NewEndpointRaw creates a new EndpointRaw struct.
24: func NewEndpointRaw(node, nodeIf, mac string) *EndpointRaw {
25: 	return &EndpointRaw{
26: 		Node:  node,
27: 		Iface: nodeIf,
28: 		MAC:   mac,
29: 	}
30: }
31: 
32: // Resolve resolves the EndpointRaw into an Endpoint interface that is implemented
33: // by a concrete endpoint struct such as EndpointBridge, EndpointHost, EndpointVeth.
34: // The type of an endpoint is determined by the node it belongs to.
35: // Resolving a raw endpoint adds an associated Link and Node to the endpoint.
36: // It also adds the endpoint to the node.
37: func (er *EndpointRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams, l Link) (Endpoint, error) {
38: 	// check if the referenced node does exist
39: 	node, exists := params.Nodes[er.Node]
40: 	if !exists {
41: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to find node %s", er.Node)
42: 	}
43: 
44: 	genericEndpoint := NewEndpointGeneric(node, er.Iface, l)
45: 
46: 	if er.IPv4 != "" {
47: 		p, err := netip.ParsePrefix(er.IPv4)
48: 		if err != nil || !p.Addr().Is4() {
49: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
50: 				"invalid ipv4 address %q for %s:%s",
51: 				er.IPv4,
52: 				er.Node,
53: 				er.Iface,
54: 			)
55: 		}
56: 		genericEndpoint.IPv4 = p
57: 	}
58: 
59: 	if er.IPv6 != "" {
60: 		p, err := netip.ParsePrefix(er.IPv6)
61: 		if err != nil || !p.Addr().Is6() {
62: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
63: 				"invalid ipv6 address %q for %s:%s",
64: 				er.IPv6,
65: 				er.Node,
66: 				er.Iface,
67: 			)
68: 		}
69: 		genericEndpoint.IPv6 = p
70: 	}
71: 
72: 	// Normalize vars to ensure JSON serialization compatibility
73: 	// (converts map[interface{}]interface{} to map[string]any)
74: 	genericEndpoint.Vars = normalizeVars(er.Vars)
75: 
76: 	var err error
77: 	if er.MAC == "" {
78: 		// if mac is not present generate one
79: 		genericEndpoint.MAC, err = clabutils.GenMac(clabconstants.ClabOUI)
80: 		if err != nil {
81: 			return nil, err
82: 		}
83: 	} else {
84: 		// if MAC is present, set it
85: 		m, err := net.ParseMAC(er.MAC)
86: 		if err != nil {
87: 			return nil, err
88: 		}
89: 		genericEndpoint.MAC = m
90: 	}
91: 
92: 	var e Endpoint
93: 
94: 	switch node.GetLinkEndpointType() {
95: 	case LinkEndpointTypeBridge, LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS:
96: 		e = NewEndpointBridge(genericEndpoint, false)
97: 
98: 	case LinkEndpointTypeHost:
99: 		e = NewEndpointHost(genericEndpoint)
100: 
101: 	case LinkEndpointTypeVeth:
102: 		e = NewEndpointVeth(genericEndpoint)
103: 	}
104: 	if l.GetType() == LinkTypeDummy {
105: 		e = NewEndpointDummy(genericEndpoint)
106: 	}
107: 	// also add the endpoint to the node
108: 	err = node.AddEndpoint(e)
109: 	if err != nil {
110: 		return nil, err
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	return e, nil
114: }

Reasoning
So for a link between a container (e.g. linux/srl node) and an `ovs-bridge` node, the ovs endpoint is an `EndpointBridge` whose node is the ovs node, and the other endpoint is an `EndpointVeth` on the container node. Let me check how a veth link deploys when one endpoint is a bridge node — `LinkVEth.Deploy` handles the bridge endpoint by calling `node.AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link, SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep))` on the bridge node (the ovs node). Let me confirm by reading link_veth.go Deploy.
read · completed

22: // ToLinkBriefRaw converts the raw link into a LinkBriefRaw.
23: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) ToLinkBriefRaw() *LinkBriefRaw {
24: 	lc := &LinkBriefRaw{
25: 		Endpoints:        []string{},
26: 		LinkCommonParams: r.LinkCommonParams,
27: 	}
28: 
29: 	for _, e := range r.Endpoints {

39: // Resolve resolves the raw veth link definition into a Link interface that is implemented
40: // by a concrete LinkVEth struct.
41: // Resolving a veth link resolves its endpoints.
42: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams) (Link, error) {
43: 	// filtered true means the link is in the filter provided by a user
44: 	// aka it should be resolved/created/deployed
45: 	filtered := isInFilter(params, r.Endpoints)
46: 	if !filtered {
47: 		return nil, nil
48: 	}
49: 
50: 	if r.fromBrief && r.briefDefaultLinkType(params) == LinkTypeVethStitch {
51: 		return NewVEthStitchedRawFromVEth(r).Resolve(params)
52: 	}
53: 
54: 	// create LinkVEth struct
55: 	l := NewLinkVEth()
56: 	l.LinkCommonParams = r.LinkCommonParams
57: 
58: 	// Normalize link vars to ensure JSON serialization compatibility
59: 	l.Vars = normalizeVars(l.Vars)
60: 
61: 	// resolve raw endpoints (epr) to endpoints (ep)
62: 	for _, epr := range r.Endpoints {
63: 		ep, err := epr.Resolve(params, l)
64: 		if err != nil {
65: 			return nil, err
66: 		}
67: 
68: 		// add endpoint to the link endpoints
69: 		l.Endpoints = append(l.Endpoints, ep)
70: 	}
71: 
72: 	// set default link mtu if MTU is unset
73: 	if l.MTU == 0 {
74: 		l.MTU = clabconstants.DefaultLinkMTU
75: 	}
76: 
77: 	return l, nil
78: }
79: 
80: // linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw creates a raw veth link from a LinkBriefRaw.
81: func linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw(lb *LinkBriefRaw) (*LinkVEthRaw, error) {
82: 	host, hostIf, node, nodeIf, err := extractHostNodeInterfaceData(lb, 0)
83: 	if err != nil {
84: 		return nil, err
85: 	}
86: 
87: 	link := &LinkVEthRaw{
88: 		LinkCommonParams: lb.LinkCommonParams,
89: 		Endpoints: []*EndpointRaw{
90: 			NewEndpointRaw(host, hostIf, ""),
91: 			NewEndpointRaw(node, nodeIf, ""),
92: 		},
93: 	}
94: 
95: 	// populate vars

97: 		return nil, err
98: 	}
99: 
100: 	// set default link mtu if MTU is unset
101: 	if link.MTU == 0 {
102: 		link.MTU = clabconstants.DefaultLinkMTU
103: 	}
104: 
105: 	return link, nil
106: }

108: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) cleanupFilteredLink(
109: 	ctx context.Context,
110: 	labName string,
111: 	nodesFilter []string,
112: ) error {
113: 	if !r.fromBrief {
114: 		return nil
115: 	}
116: 
117: 	// probe every brief veth because its node-selected default is
118: 	// unavailable after filtering; non-stitched probes are harmless no-ops.
119: 	return cleanupFilteredVethStitch(ctx, labName, nodesFilter, r.Endpoints)
120: }

122: // defaultLinkTypeProvider is a type that can provide the default link type for a node. Currently
123: // only needed for SR-SIM nodes that use veth-stitch for a regular veth link.
124: type defaultLinkTypeProvider interface {
125: 	DefaultLinkType() LinkType
126: }
127: 
128: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) briefDefaultLinkType(params *ResolveParams) LinkType {
129: 	for _, ep := range r.Endpoints {
130: 		n, ok := params.Nodes[ep.Node]
131: 		if !ok {
132: 			continue
133: 		}
134: 
135: 		provider, ok := n.(defaultLinkTypeProvider)
136: 		if !ok {
137: 			continue
138: 		}
139: 
140: 		lt := provider.DefaultLinkType()
141: 		if lt != "" && lt != LinkTypeVEth {
142: 			return lt
143: 		}
144: 	}
145: 
146: 	return LinkTypeVEth
147: }

149: type LinkVEth struct {
150: 	LinkCommonParams
151: 	Endpoints []Endpoint
152: 
153: 	deployMutex sync.Mutex
154: }
155: 
156: func NewLinkVEth() *LinkVEth {
157: 	return &LinkVEth{
158: 		Endpoints: make([]Endpoint, 0, 2),
159: 	}
160: }
161: 
162: func (*LinkVEth) GetType() LinkType {
163: 	return LinkTypeVEth
164: }
165: 
166: func (l *LinkVEth) deployAEnd(ctx context.Context, idx int) error {
167: 	ep := l.Endpoints[idx]
168: 	// the peer Endpoint is the other of the two endpoints in the
169: 	// Endpoints slice. So do a +1 on the index and modulo operation
170: 	// to take care of the wrap around.
171: 	peerIdx := (idx + 1) % 2
172: 	peerEp := l.Endpoints[peerIdx]
173: 
174: 	log.Debugf("Creating Endpoint: %s ( --> %s )", ep, peerEp)
175: 
176: 	// build the netlink.Veth struct for the link provisioning
177: 	linkA := &netlink.Veth{
178: 		LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{
179: 			Name: ep.GetRandIfaceName(),
180: 			MTU:  l.MTU,
181: 			// Mac address is set later on
182: 		},
183: 		PeerName: peerEp.GetRandIfaceName(),
184: 		// PeerMac address is set later on
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	// add the link
188: 	err := netlink.LinkAdd(linkA)
189: 	if err != nil {
190: 		return err
191: 	}
192: 
193: 	// cleanup is a helper that removes the veth pair if a subsequent step fails
194: 	// after LinkAdd succeeded, preventing orphaned interfaces from blocking retries.
195: 	cleanup := func(err error) error {
196: 		if delErr := netlink.LinkDel(linkA); delErr != nil {
197: 			log.Debugf(
198: 				"failed to cleanup veth pair %s after error: %v",
199: 				ep.GetRandIfaceName(),
200: 				delErr,
201: 			)
202: 		}
203: 		return err
204: 	}
205: 
206: 	// disable TXOffloading
207: 	if err := clabutils.EthtoolTXOff(ep.GetRandIfaceName()); err != nil {
208: 		return cleanup(err)
209: 	}
210: 
211: 	// the link needs to be moved to the relevant network namespace
212: 	// and enabled (up). This is done via linkSetupFunc.
213: 	// based on the endpoint type the link setup function is different.
214: 	// linkSetupFunc is executed in a netns of a node.
215: 	// if the node is a regular namespace node
216: 	// add link to node, rename, set mac and Up
217: 	err = ep.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, linkA,
218: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(linkA, ep))
219: 	if err != nil {
220: 		return cleanup(err)
221: 	}
222: 
223: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed
224: 
225: 	// e.g. host endpoints are nodeless, and therefore the B end of the veth link should
226: 	// be deployed right after the A end is deployed.
227: 	if peerEp.IsNodeless() {
228: 		return l.deployBEnd(ctx, peerIdx)
229: 	}
230: 
231: 	return nil
232: }
233: 
234: func (l *LinkVEth) deployBEnd(ctx context.Context, idx int) error {
235: 	ep := l.Endpoints[idx]
236: 	peerEp := l.Endpoints[(idx+1)%2]
237: 
238: 	log.Debugf("Assigning Endpoint: %s ( --> %s )", ep, peerEp)
239: 
240: 	// retrieve the netlink.Link for the provided Endpoint
241: 	link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ep.GetRandIfaceName())
242: 	if err != nil {
243: 		return err
244: 	}
245: 
246: 	// disable TXOffloading
247: 	if err := clabutils.EthtoolTXOff(ep.GetRandIfaceName()); err != nil {
248: 		return err
249: 	}
250: 
251: 	// Host endpoints already live in the current namespace; set them up in place.
252: 	// Other nodeless endpoints, such as the management bridge, still need their
253: 	// node-specific setup.
254: 	if ep.IsNodeless() && ep.GetNode().GetLinkEndpointType() == LinkEndpointTypeHost {
255: 		if err := SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep)(nil); err != nil {
256: 			return err
257: 		}
258: 	} else if err = ep.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link,
259: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep)); err != nil {
260: 		return err
261: 	}
262: 
263: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed
264: 
265: 	if len(l.Endpoints) == 2 {
266: 		log.Infof("Created link: %s ▪┄┄▪ %s", l.Endpoints[0], l.Endpoints[1])
267: 	}
268: 
269: 	return nil
270: }
271: 
272: // getEndpointIndex returns the index of the ep endpoint belonging to l link.
273: // An error is returned when the ep is not part of the l's endpoints.
274: func (l *LinkVEth) getEndpointIndex(ep Endpoint) (int, error) {
275: 	for idx, e := range l.Endpoints {
276: 		if e == ep {
277: 			return idx, nil
278: 		}
279: 	}
280: 
281: 	// if the endpoint is not part of the link
282: 	// build a string list of endpoints and return a meaningful error
283: 	var epStrings []string
284: 	for _, e := range l.Endpoints {
285: 		epStrings = append(epStrings, e.String())
286: 	}
287: 
288: 	return -1, fmt.Errorf(
289: 		"endpoint %s does not belong to link [ %s ]",
290: 		ep.String(),
291: 		strings.Join(epStrings, ", "),
292: 	)
293: }
294: 
295: // Deploy deploys the veth link by creating the A and B sides of the veth pair independently
296: // based on the calling endpoint.
297: func (l *LinkVEth) Deploy(ctx context.Context, ep Endpoint) error {
298: 	// since each node calls deploy on its links, we need to make sure that we only deploy
299: 	// the link once, even if multiple nodes call deploy on the same link.
300: 	l.deployMutex.Lock()
301: 	defer l.deployMutex.Unlock()
302: 
303: 	// first we need to check that the provided ep is part of this link
304: 	idx, err := l.getEndpointIndex(ep)
305: 	if err != nil {
306: 		return err
307: 	}
308: 
309: 	// Retry transient netlink errors that can occur during concurrent link operations.
310: 	// deployAEnd cleans up partial state on failure, making each retry idempotent.
311: 	return retryTransientNetlink(ctx, fmt.Sprintf("deploy link %s", ep), func() error {
312: 		switch l.DeploymentState {
313: 		case LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed:
314: 			return l.deployBEnd(ctx, idx)
315: 		case LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed:
316: 			return nil
317: 		default:
318: 			return l.deployAEnd(ctx, idx)
319: 		}
320: 	})
321: }
322: 
323: func (*LinkVEth) PostDeploy(context.Context) error {
324: 	return nil
325: }
326: 
327: func (l *LinkVEth) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
328: 	l.deployMutex.Lock()
329: 	defer l.deployMutex.Unlock()
330: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
331: 		return nil
332: 	}
333: 	for _, ep := range l.GetEndpoints() {
334: 		err := ep.Remove(ctx)
335: 		if err != nil {
336: 			log.Debug(err)
337: 		}
338: 	}
339: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateRemoved
340: 	return nil
341: }
342: 
343: func (l *LinkVEth) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
344: 	return l.Endpoints
345: }
346: 
347: func (l *LinkVEth) GetRuntimeEndpoints() []Endpoint {
348: 	return l.GetEndpoints()
349: }

(End of file - total 349 lines)

9: -{{ kind_display_name }}- is identified with `-{{ kind_code_name }}-` kind in the [topology file](../topo-def-file.md).
10: 
11: Similar to [linux bridge](bridge.md) capability, containerlab allows to connect nodes to an Openvswitch (Ovs) bridge. Ovs bridges offers even more connectivity options compared to classic Linux bridge, as well as it allows to create stretched L2 domain by means of tunneled interfaces (vxlan).
12: 
13: ## Using ovs-bridge kind
14: Containerlab doesn't create bridges on users behalf, that means that in order to use a bridge in the [topology definition file](../topo-def-file.md), the Ovs bridge needs to be created first.
15: 
16: Once the bridge is created, it has to be referenced as a node inside the topology file:
17: 
18: ```yaml
19: name: ovs
20: 
21: topology:
22:   nodes:
23:     myovs:
24:       kind: -{{ kind_code_name }}-
25:     ceos:
26:       kind: arista_ceos
27:       image: ceos:latest
28:   links:
29:     - endpoints: ["myovs:ovsp1", "srl:eth1"]
30: 
31: ```
32: 
33: In the example above, node `myovs` of kind `-{{ kind_code_name }}-` tells containerlab to identify it as a Ovs bridge and look for a bridge named `myovs`.
34: 
35: When connecting other nodes to a bridge, the bridge endpoint must be present in the `links` section.
36: 
37: !!!note
38:     When choosing names of the interfaces that need to be connected to the bridge make sure that these names are not clashing with existing interfaces.  
39:     In the example above we attach a single interfaces named `ovsp1` to the Ovs bridge with a name `myovs`. Before that, we ensured that no other interfaces named `ovsp1` existed.
40: 
41: As a result of such topology definition, you will see the Ovs bridge `br-clab` with three interfaces attached to it:
42:

Reasoning
So for the ovs-bridge node, the endpoint ifaceName in the topology is the OVS port name (`ovsp1`), which is the name of the netdev that gets renamed to that. Wait, in the example, `Port eth1 / Interface ovsp1` — hmm. Anyway.

The bug being fixed: when the topology interface name for the OVS port is > 15 chars (or invalid as Linux ifname), the netdev keeps the random `clab-xxxxxxxx` name and the topology name is set as altname. OVS records the port under the netdev name (`clab-xxxxxxxx`). Then on destroy, `c.VSwitch.DeletePort(bridge, ep.GetIfaceName())` deletes the port named after the topology name — which doesn't exist. So the OVS port leaks. This PR fixes by tagging and cleanup.

Now let me trace `logicalEndpointName`: it iterates endpoints of the ovs node, comparing `ep.GetIfaceName()` to link name or altname. That works if the netdev name is `clab-xxxxxxxx` and altname is sanitized iface name.

Potential issues to examine:

1. `SanitizeInterfaceName` in `EndpointGeneric.Remove` and `RemoveOwnedInterface`: `netlink.LinkByName(clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(e.GetIfaceName()))`. Why was this added? Because the altname is the sanitized name, so if the topology iface name has a `/` or ` ` (invalid in ifname), the altname is sanitized. So `LinkByName(sanitized)` finds the netdev by altname. But wait — `LinkByName` in netlink library looks up by name AND altname? Actually, Go netlink's `LinkByName` does lookup by name; I recall vishvananda/netlink supports altname lookup: `LinkByName` checks `altnames` table. Yes — netlink.LinkByName handles alt names via `newLinkMsg` lookup... Actually the netlink library added altname support: `LinkLookupByName` checks altnames. OK.

But hold on: in `EndpointGeneric.Remove`, previously it was `netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())` without sanitization. The altname set in `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` is the sanitized name. So for names with '/' or ' ', the lookup previously failed → `notfound` → return nil (no-op, leak). Now sanitized lookup finds it. Good.

But note: `CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet`, `Activate`, `ensureOwnershipAltName`, `ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor`, `moveLink` still use the unsanitized name. Inconsistency? Those may be pre-existing. The PR only touches Remove paths. Not necessarily a defect introduced by PR, but the PR's sanitization is incomplete across sibling callers — per ponytail rule: "Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once". Hmm, but `LinkByName` with unsanitized name: does netlink lookup match altnames? The altname stored is the sanitized one, so an unsanitized lookup would fail to find it. That means `Activate` would fail for such names... but that's pre-existing behavior, not introduced here. Actually wait — for names that are valid (no '/' or ' '), sanitized == original, so no change. Only invalid-name endpoints changed. Those endpoints' `Remove` now works. Other functions still fail — pre-existing.

2. `Remove` in endpoint.go: on `notfound`, it calls `removeBridgePort(e.GetNode(), e.GetIfaceName())`. The node here is the container node (for the container-side endpoint) or... wait, which endpoint's Remove triggers? `LinkVEth.Remove` iterates all endpoints and calls `ep.Remove(ctx)`. For the ovs bridge endpoint (EndpointBridge on the ovs node), `EndpointGeneric.Remove` executes `ExecFunction` in the ovs node's namespace — which is the root namespace (IsRootNamespaceBased). So `netlink.LinkByName(sanitized ifaceName)` in the root ns: for the ovs bridge endpoint, the ifaceName is the OVS port name. Hmm wait — the endpoint on the ovs node: its ifaceName is the topology port name (e.g., `ovsp1`). If the port name is valid, the netdev got renamed to it, so LinkByName succeeds and LinkDel deletes the netdev → OVS removes the port automatically. If invalid (>15 chars), the netdev is `clab-xxxxxxxx` and altname is the sanitized name; LinkByName(sanitized) finds the netdev via altname, deletes it → OVS port auto-removed by kernel. Good.

But then `removeBridgePort(node=ovsNode, ifaceName)` — the ovs node implements `DeleteBridgePort`, so it calls `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` which finds OVS interfaces tagged with `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=<logical>` and deletes the port. After the netdev was deleted, the OVS port is also auto-deleted by OVS (when the interface netdev disappears, OVS removes the interface... actually when the netdev disappears, ovs-vswitchd marks the interface with error "could not open network device" but the Port stays! That's exactly the bug being fixed: the Port remains with an Interface in error state). So `removeEndpointPorts` finds the port (it still exists in ovsdb, with the tag) and deletes it. 

Wait — but if the netdev is named `clab-xxxxxxxx`, the OVS Port is named `clab-xxxxxxxx`, and the Interface row is `clab-xxxxxxxx` with external_ids tag = logical name. `removeEndpointPorts` finds by tag, deletes. Good.

But there's a subtlety: `EndpointGeneric.Remove` is invoked for the ovs node's endpoint AND the container endpoint. For the container endpoint, `removeBridgePort(containerNode, ...)` — container node doesn't implement `DeleteBridgePort` → no-op. Fine.

Now, when the container-side netdev is deleted first (LinkDel succeeds) — the peer netdev on the OVS side disappears. Then the ovs endpoint Remove: `LinkByName(sanitized)` — notfound → `removeBridgePort(ovsNode, ifaceName)` → removes tagged port. 

But wait — there's an ordering issue: what if the ovs endpoint's Remove runs first and the netdev is found (still exists because peer side not yet deleted)? Then LinkDel deletes the ovs-side netdev → OVS port becomes error-stale; then `removeBridgePort` removes the tagged port. OK.

Now, what about `RemoveOwnedInterface` called from `core/apply.go` and `core/topology_reconcile.go` — with `ep.GetNode()` and `ep.GetIfaceName()` — for runtime endpoints of the link. Same logic.

3. Now the `Delete` method in ovs.go: loops endpoints calling `DeletePort(bridge, ep.GetIfaceName())`. Hmm — wait. This deletes the port named after the topology iface name. But the port might be named `clab-xxxxxxxx` (invalid iface name) — then DeletePort with the logical name is a no-op (--if-exists). Then `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` removes the tagged port. Good.

Then `removeMissingAutoPorts()` sweep: lists ports, matches `^clab-[0-9a-f]{8}$`, checks if netdev missing via `ovs-vsctl get Interface <port> error`, deletes if missing.

Wait — potential issue: `genRandomIfName` = "clab-" + genRandomString(8) where genRandomString takes the first 8 bytes of a UUID text (hex chars 0-9a-f). So `clab-[0-9a-f]{8}` matches. But other containerlab-generated random netdev names could also be `clab-*`? Random names are used for all endpoints (`GetRandIfaceName`) — e.g., for a veth pair in a lab with invalid interface names, the netdev name could be `clab-xxxxxxxx`. Are these random names visible as OVS ports? Only if attached to OVS. But could an operator's own interface named `clab-1234abcd` exist on the OVS bridge with a missing netdev? Unlikely but possible — the sweep only removes ports whose netdev is missing, so an operator's own `clab-*` port with a live netdev is safe. A missing operator port named `clab-*`... edge. The comment says "Untagged ports may belong to the operator, so only Delete removes a port by its name." The sweep does delete untagged ports matching the regex if the device is missing. Small risk; matches deliberate tradeoff. Could be a P4 note or not worth flagging. Actually the comment in DeleteBridgePort says "Untagged ports may belong to the operator, so only Delete removes a port by its name" — and the sweep does remove untagged clab-* ports during Delete. The regex constrains to clab-8-hex which is very much clab-generated. Fine.

4. Now the `AddLinkToContainer` change: on attach, if `logical != name`, it first calls `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` — which runs `ovs-vsctl find Interface external_ids:...` and `ListPorts`, then deletes tagged ports on the bridge. Then adds the port with the tag via `ovs-vsctl --may-exist add-port ... -- set Interface ... external_ids`.

Potential issue: `removeEndpointPorts` will delete ANY interface on ANY bridge with the tag? No — it filters by `onBridge` set from ListPorts of this bridge. But `ovsInterfaceNames` searches globally across all bridges for interfaces with that external_ids tag. Two different OVS bridges in the same lab, each with an endpoint with the same logical name (e.g., two ovs bridges each with endpoint `eth1`)? Then attach to bridge A removes the tagged port on bridge A only (filtered by onBridge). OK.

But: external_ids on the Interface row vs Port row. The tag is set on `Interface` table row. `--may-exist add-port bridge portname` creates Port + Interface of the same name. The `set Interface name external_ids:...` sets on Interface. When the port is deleted and re-added with the same name (--may-exist), the Interface row is recreated fresh (no tag) — that's why on each attach when logical != name, they removeEndpointPorts first then re-add with the tag. OK.

Now a subtle bug in AddLinkToContainer: `--may-exist add-port` with the netdev name `clab-xxxxxxxx`. Each attach uses a fresh random name, so no clash. But what if two endpoints in the same lab with the same logical name attach to the same bridge? Only one endpoint per logical name per node (endpoint uniqueness check). OK.

What about deploy failure and retry: `deployAEnd` cleanup deletes the veth pair on error, and retries (retryTransientNetlink). If `AddLinkToContainer` fails after `removeEndpointPorts` succeeded... e.g. ovs-vsctl add-port fails → error → cleanup deletes the veth → retry creates a NEW veth with a new random name, calls AddLinkToContainer again → removeEndpointPorts(logical) (already clean) → add-port with new name + tag. Fine.

But wait: there's a subtle leak: in `deployAEnd`, `AddLinkToContainer` is called with `SetNameMACAndUpInterface(linkA, ep)` — the rename to logical name happens inside `curNamespace.Do(f)`. For the OVS node, `AddLinkToContainer` executes f in the current namespace (root), renames netdev to logical name IF valid, else adds altname. Then re-reads link by index. OK.

5. Concurrency: `AddLinkToContainer` for ovs: `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` — if two links to the same bridge deploy concurrently with different logical names, no conflict. Same logical name — uniqueness prevents. But `LinkVEth.deployMutex` per link; different links could deploy concurrently. removeEndpointPorts on same bridge with different tags — each removes only its own tag. But wait — there's a race: `removeEndpointPorts` for link A runs `find` + `ListPorts` + `DeletePort`, and link B concurrently... A's find might see B's interface, but B's interface isn't on A's tag, so no cross-deletion. OK.

However, there is a real race: `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` deletes tagged ports, then `ovsVsctl add-port` adds new port. If two concurrent deploys of the SAME logical endpoint (redeploy)... uniqueness.

6. Now, `ovsMapValue`: converts value for ovsdb map value syntax. Values with special chars get `%q` (Go quoting). But `%q` produces Go-style quoting which differs from ovsdb string quoting for some characters (e.g. non-ASCII runes: Go escapes as `\u`? Actually `%q` escapes non-ASCII printable runes as-is, control chars as \uXXXX; ovsdb expects JSON strings — mostly compatible). But a bigger issue: the `find` condition `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=ovsMapValue(endpoint)` — for an endpoint name with a space, ovsMapValue returns `"has space"` — is that valid ovs-vsctl syntax for a map value in a `find` condition? `find Interface external_ids:containerlab-endpoint="has space"` — ovs-vsctl conditions: comparing string values requires double quotes. Yes valid.

But there's an injection concern: ifaceName comes from the topology file. If iface name contains `"` or `;`, could that inject ovs-vsctl arguments? `ovsVsctl` uses `exec.Command("ovs-vsctl", args...)` — args are separate argv entries, so shell injection is not possible. But ovs-vsctl itself parses the condition argument; the condition string is a single argv element: `"find", "Interface", condition` where condition = `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint="has space"`. If ifaceName contains a `"`, `%q` escapes it — good. If ifaceName contains `==` or other condition operators, ovsMapValue only quotes when non-alnum/-/_. chars present. Characters like `=` are not in the allowed set → quoted. Actually wait, `:` is allowed unquoted. `@` allowed. Hmm, `:` in a value... the condition is `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=<value>`; a `:` in the value is fine within the value part. OK.

But what about `%q` producing something ovs-vsctl can't parse for weird characters (e.g. backslash sequences)? Go `%q` escapes `"` as `\"`, backslash as `\\`, control chars as `\n` etc. OVSDB string literals support `\"`, `\\`, `\n`? OVSDB supports JSON escape sequences — yes ovsdb strings are JSON strings, so `\n`, `\uXXXX` are valid. Go `%q` escapes some chars differently: e.g. `\x01` → Go `\x01` but JSON expects `\u0001`. Minor edge; interface names with control chars are unrealistic. Skip.

Also: ovsMapValue is applied to a value that should be an ovsdb map VALUE — external_ids:containerlab-endpoint is a map column; condition `external_ids:key=value` matches map values. Fine.

7. `ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing`: uses `ovs-vsctl get Interface <iface> error`. If the interface exists and is live, error column is `[]` and command succeeds. If the netdev is missing, the error column contains e.g. "could not open network device clab-deadbeef (No such device)" — and ovs-vsctl `get` of a string column value containing spaces with `--data=bare` prints the string without quotes. Exit code 0. Good. If row missing, ovs-vsctl errors with "no row ... in table Interface" on stderr — CombinedOutput captures it. Good.

Hmm, wait: does `get Interface <name> error` fail when the Interface exists but... fine.

8. Now the important correctness question in `Delete`:

```go
err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, ep.GetIfaceName())
if err == nil {
    err = n.removeEndpointPorts(ep.GetIfaceName())
}
```

Why check `err == nil` before removeEndpointPorts? If DeletePort fails (e.g. ovsdb down), skip removeEndpointPorts. OK.

But there's a deeper issue: `Delete` is per-node and runs when destroying the lab. `removeEndpointPorts` deletes ports tagged for this endpoint across... filtered to this bridge. OK.

9. Now — the stale-port sweep `removeMissingAutoPorts` is only run in `Delete` (ovs node delete). But consider `lab destroy` path: does the ovs node's `Delete` get called before/after containers stop? The comment says the per-endpoint deletion already removes ports tagged at attach time; the sweep handles ports created before they were tagged (i.e., created by an older containerlab version that didn't tag). So after upgrading containerlab, destroying a lab from the old version: the ports are named `clab-*` (netdev gone) — the sweep removes them. But what if the netdev still exists (container still running)? Old-version ports named after the topology interface (valid names) are deleted by `DeletePort(bridge, ep.GetIfaceName())`. Old-version ports for invalid names are `clab-*` with live netdevs... wait if the netdev exists it's attached to the bridge; during destroy, when the container stops, its netdevs disappear. Order matters: ovs node Delete might run before container deletion → netdev still present → not missing → not deleted → leak persists. Hmm. But `--if-exists` DeletePort by iface name fails for old-style ports. Let me check the destroy order: nodes are deleted (containers stopped) and links removed. Let me check how destroy proceeds — does link removal (RemoveOwnedInterface) run after containers are gone? For destroy: `c.destroy` iterates nodes deleting containers, then links? Let me check cmd/root destroy flow.

Actually for the upgrade path: old containerlab deployed the lab with invalid iface names → OVS port `clab-xxxxxxxx` untagged, netdev live. New containerlab runs `destroy`. During destroy, the container-side veth peer deletion happens via RemoveOwnedInterface (netdev gone? no, container still running until container delete). Now, a crucial question: in `RemoveOwnedInterface`/`removeBridgePort`, the node for the OVS-side endpoint is the ovs node — good, it implements `DeleteBridgePort`. But is the ovs endpoint's ifaceName = the topology interface name (port name)? Yes, per ovs_test and `logicalEndpointName`. But wait — for a real lab, when a link is `r1:eth1 -- myovs:ovsp1`, the ovs endpoint iface is `ovsp1`. Good.

But wait, there's a discrepancy: what's the OVS endpoint's `ifaceName` when the OVS node is used as a plain bridge with the node shortname? E.g., `endpoints: ["myovs:ovsp1", "srl:eth1"]`. OK.
